void Diagnose(RecommendationCampaignStatusMember& member, std::string code)
{
    if (std::find(member.diagnosticCodes.begin(), member.diagnosticCodes.end(), code) ==
        member.diagnosticCodes.end())
        member.diagnosticCodes.push_back(std::move(code));
    member.consistency = RecommendationCampaignStatusConsistency::inconsistent;
    member.operationalState = RecommendationCampaignStatusOperationalState::inconsistent;
    member.terminalResult = RecommendationCampaignStatusTerminalResult::unknown;
}

bool IsKnownStatus(const std::string& value)
{
    return value == "pending" || value == "paused" || value == "running" ||
        value == "completed" || value == "failed" || value == "cancelled";
}

bool IsKnownPhase(const std::string& value)
{
    return value == "train" || value == "infer" || value == "analyze" ||
        value == "done";
}

RecommendationCampaignStatusMember ClassifyMember(
    const RecommendationCampaignStatusMemberInput& input)
{
    RecommendationCampaignStatusMember member;
    member.memberOrdinal = input.memberOrdinal;
    member.materializationMemberId = input.materializationMemberId;
    member.recommendationId = input.recommendationId;
    member.sourceExperimentId = input.sourceExperimentId;
    member.rankingMemberId = input.rankingMemberId;
    member.proposalId = input.proposalId;
    member.currentReviewDecisionId = input.currentReviewDecisionId;
    member.currentReviewDecision = input.currentReviewDecision;
    member.authorizationReviewDecisionId = input.authorizationReviewDecisionId;
    member.executionId = input.executionId;
    member.activationId = input.activationId;
    member.experimentId = input.experimentId;
    member.executionEvidenceCount = input.executionCount;
    member.activationEvidenceCount = input.activationCount;

    if (input.executionCount > 1)
        member.executionState = RecommendationCampaignStatusExecutionState::conflicting;
    else if (input.executionId)
        member.executionState = RecommendationCampaignStatusExecutionState::executed;
    if (input.activationCount > 1)
        member.activationState = RecommendationCampaignStatusActivationState::conflicting;
    else if (input.activationId)
        member.activationState = RecommendationCampaignStatusActivationState::activated;

    if (!input.workflowConsistent)
    {
        if (input.executionCount == 1 && input.executionId)
            member.executionState = RecommendationCampaignStatusExecutionState::malformed;
        if (input.activationCount == 1 && input.activationId)
            member.activationState = RecommendationCampaignStatusActivationState::malformed;
        for (const auto& code : input.workflowDiagnostics) Diagnose(member, code);
        if (input.workflowDiagnostics.empty())
            Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_workflow_inconsistent");
    }
    if (input.executionCount < 0 || input.executionCount > 1 ||
        (input.executionId.has_value() != (input.executionCount == 1)))
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_execution_cardinality_invalid");
    if (input.activationCount < 0 || input.activationCount > 1 ||
        (input.activationId.has_value() != (input.activationCount == 1)))
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_activation_cardinality_invalid");
    if (input.activationId && !input.executionId)
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_activation_without_execution");
    if (!input.executionId)
    {
        if (input.experiment || input.experimentId)
            Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_experiment_without_execution");
        return member;
    }
    if (!input.experiment || !input.experimentId)
    {
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_execution_experiment_missing");
        return member;
    }

    const auto& experiment = *input.experiment;
    member.symbol = experiment.symbol;
    member.predictionHorizon = experiment.predictionHorizon;
    member.status = experiment.status;
    member.phase = experiment.phase;
    member.currentEpoch = experiment.currentEpoch;
    member.targetEpochs = experiment.targetEpochs;
    member.workerPid = experiment.workerPid;
    member.currentOperation = experiment.currentOperation;
    member.workerStartedAt = experiment.workerStartedAt;
    member.startedAt = experiment.startedAt;
    member.completedAt = experiment.completedAt;
    member.exitCode = experiment.exitCode;
    member.errorMessage = experiment.errorMessage;
    member.modelId = experiment.modelId;
    if (!experiment.invocationIdentityCanonical.empty())
        member.invocationIdentityHash = RecommendationCanonicalHash(
            experiment.invocationIdentityCanonical);
    member.modelLinkCount = experiment.modelLinkCount;
    member.finalInferenceResultCount = experiment.inferenceResultCount;
    member.completedFinalInferenceResultCount =
        experiment.completedInferenceResultCount;
    member.failedFinalInferenceResultCount =
        experiment.failedInferenceResultCount;
    member.finalAnalysisResultCount = experiment.analysisResultCount;
    member.completedFinalAnalysisResultCount =
        experiment.completedAnalysisResultCount;
    member.failedFinalAnalysisResultCount = experiment.failedAnalysisResultCount;
    member.inferConfigured = experiment.inferConfigured;
    member.analyzeConfigured = experiment.inferConfigured;
    member.inferComplete = experiment.completedInferenceResultCount > 0;
    member.analyzeComplete = experiment.completedAnalysisResultCount > 0;
    member.trainComplete = experiment.modelId.has_value() &&
        experiment.modelLinkCount == 1;

    if (experiment.experimentId != *input.experimentId)
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_experiment_provenance_mismatch");
    if (!experiment.invocationProvenanceValid)
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_experiment_invocation_mismatch");
    if (experiment.symbol.empty() || experiment.predictionHorizon <= 0 ||
        experiment.targetEpochs <= 0 || experiment.duplicateNonce != 0 ||
        experiment.invocationMode != "recommendation_conversion")
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_experiment_identity_invalid");
    if (!IsKnownStatus(experiment.status))
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_experiment_status_unknown");
    if (!IsKnownPhase(experiment.phase))
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_experiment_phase_unknown");
    if (experiment.currentEpoch &&
        (*experiment.currentEpoch < 0 || *experiment.currentEpoch > experiment.targetEpochs))
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_current_epoch_invalid");
    if (experiment.inferStartPresent != experiment.inferEndPresent)
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_inference_configuration_invalid");
    if (experiment.modelLinkCount < 0 || experiment.modelLinkCount > 1)
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_model_link_conflict");
    if (experiment.inferenceResultCount < 0 ||
        experiment.completedInferenceResultCount < 0 ||
        experiment.failedInferenceResultCount < 0 ||
        experiment.completedInferenceResultCount +
                experiment.failedInferenceResultCount !=
            experiment.inferenceResultCount ||
        experiment.completedInferenceResultCount > 1 ||
        (experiment.completedInferenceResultCount > 0 &&
         experiment.failedInferenceResultCount > 0))
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_inference_evidence_invalid");
    if (experiment.analysisResultCount < 0 ||
        experiment.completedAnalysisResultCount < 0 ||
        experiment.failedAnalysisResultCount < 0 ||
        experiment.completedAnalysisResultCount +
                experiment.failedAnalysisResultCount !=
            experiment.analysisResultCount ||
        experiment.completedAnalysisResultCount > 1 ||
        experiment.failedAnalysisResultCount > 1 ||
        (experiment.completedAnalysisResultCount > 0 &&
         experiment.failedAnalysisResultCount > 0))
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_analysis_evidence_invalid");

    if (member.consistency == RecommendationCampaignStatusConsistency::inconsistent)
        return member;

    if (!input.activationId)
    {
        if (experiment.status == "paused" && experiment.phase == "train" &&
            !experiment.workerPid && !experiment.currentOperation &&
            !experiment.currentEpoch && !experiment.workerStartedAt &&
            !experiment.startedAt && !experiment.completedAt &&
            !experiment.exitCode && !experiment.errorMessage && !experiment.modelId)
            member.operationalState = RecommendationCampaignStatusOperationalState::paused;
        else
            Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_unactivated_lifecycle_invalid");
        return member;
    }

    if (experiment.status == "pending")
    {
        if (experiment.workerPid)
            Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_pending_worker_invalid");
        else if (experiment.phase == "train" || experiment.phase == "infer" ||
                 experiment.phase == "analyze")
            member.operationalState = RecommendationCampaignStatusOperationalState::pending;
        else
            Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_pending_phase_invalid");
    }
    else if (experiment.status == "running")
    {
        if (!experiment.workerStartedAt || !experiment.currentOperation ||
            *experiment.currentOperation != experiment.phase)
            Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_running_worker_evidence_invalid");
        else if (experiment.phase == "train")
            member.operationalState = RecommendationCampaignStatusOperationalState::runningTrain;
        else if (experiment.phase == "infer")
            member.operationalState = RecommendationCampaignStatusOperationalState::runningInfer;
        else if (experiment.phase == "analyze")
            member.operationalState = RecommendationCampaignStatusOperationalState::runningAnalyze;
        else
            Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_running_phase_invalid");
    }
    else if (experiment.status == "paused")
    {
        if (experiment.startedAt || experiment.currentEpoch || experiment.modelId)
            member.operationalState =
                RecommendationCampaignStatusOperationalState::pausedAfterProgress;
        else
            Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_activated_experiment_still_paused");
    }
    else if (experiment.status == "completed")
    {
        if (experiment.phase != "done" || !experiment.completedAt ||
            experiment.workerPid || !member.trainComplete ||
            !member.inferConfigured || !member.inferComplete ||
            !member.analyzeConfigured || !member.analyzeComplete ||
            (experiment.errorMessage && !experiment.errorMessage->empty()) ||
            (experiment.exitCode && *experiment.exitCode != 0))
            Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_completed_evidence_invalid");
        else
        {
            member.operationalState = RecommendationCampaignStatusOperationalState::completed;
            member.terminalResult = RecommendationCampaignStatusTerminalResult::succeeded;
        }
    }
    else if (experiment.status == "failed")
    {
        if (!experiment.completedAt || !experiment.errorMessage ||
            experiment.errorMessage->empty() || experiment.workerPid)
            Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_failed_evidence_invalid");
        else
        {
            member.operationalState = RecommendationCampaignStatusOperationalState::failed;
            member.terminalResult = RecommendationCampaignStatusTerminalResult::failed;
        }
    }
    else if (experiment.status == "cancelled")
    {
        if (!experiment.completedAt || experiment.workerPid)
            Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_cancelled_evidence_invalid");
        else
        {
            member.operationalState = RecommendationCampaignStatusOperationalState::cancelled;
            member.terminalResult = RecommendationCampaignStatusTerminalResult::cancelled;
        }
    }
    else if (experiment.status != "pending" && experiment.status != "running" &&
             experiment.status != "paused")
        Diagnose(member, "campaign_status_experiment_lifecycle_invalid");

    return member;
}

std::string OptionalLongLong(const std::optional<long long>& value)
{
    return value ? std::to_string(*value) : "null";
}

std::string OptionalInt(const std::optional<int>& value)
{
    return value ? std::to_string(*value) : "null";
}

std::string OptionalText(const std::optional<std::string>& value)
{
    return value ? std::to_string(value->size()) + ":" + *value : "null";
}

std::string SnapshotCanonical(
    const RecommendationCampaignStatusInput& input,
    const std::vector<RecommendationCampaignStatusMember>& members)
{
    std::ostringstream out;
    out.imbue(std::locale::classic());
    out << "experiment_recommendation_campaign_status_snapshot_v1"
        << ";status_contract_version=" << kRecommendationCampaignStatusContractVersion
        << ";materialization_id=" << input.materializationId
        << ";materialization_contract_version=" << input.materializationContractVersion
        << ";materialization_identity_hash=" << input.materializationIdentityHash
        << ";member_count=" << members.size();
    for (const auto& member : members)
    {
        out << ";member=" << member.memberOrdinal << ','
            << member.materializationMemberId << ',' << member.recommendationId << ','
            << member.sourceExperimentId << ',' << member.rankingMemberId << ','
            << member.proposalId << ','
            << OptionalLongLong(member.currentReviewDecisionId) << ','
            << OptionalText(member.currentReviewDecision) << ','
            << OptionalLongLong(member.authorizationReviewDecisionId) << ','
            << OptionalLongLong(member.executionId) << ','
            << OptionalLongLong(member.activationId) << ','
            << OptionalLongLong(member.experimentId) << ','
            << member.executionEvidenceCount << ','
            << member.activationEvidenceCount << ','
            << RecommendationCampaignStatusExecutionStateText(member.executionState)
            << ',' << RecommendationCampaignStatusActivationStateText(member.activationState)
            << ',' << RecommendationCampaignStatusOperationalStateText(member.operationalState)
            << ',' << RecommendationCampaignStatusTerminalResultText(member.terminalResult)
            << ',' << OptionalText(member.symbol) << ','
            << OptionalInt(member.predictionHorizon) << ','
            << OptionalText(member.status) << ',' << OptionalText(member.phase)
            << ',' << OptionalInt(member.currentEpoch) << ','
            << OptionalInt(member.targetEpochs) << ',' << OptionalInt(member.workerPid)
            << ',' << OptionalText(member.currentOperation) << ','
            << OptionalText(member.workerStartedAt) << ','
            << OptionalText(member.startedAt) << ','
            << OptionalText(member.completedAt) << ','
            << OptionalInt(member.exitCode) << ','
            << OptionalText(member.errorMessage) << ','
            << OptionalLongLong(member.modelId) << ','
            << OptionalText(member.invocationIdentityHash) << ','
            << member.modelLinkCount << ','
            << member.finalInferenceResultCount << ','
            << member.completedFinalInferenceResultCount << ','
            << member.failedFinalInferenceResultCount << ','
            << member.finalAnalysisResultCount << ','
            << member.completedFinalAnalysisResultCount << ','
            << member.failedFinalAnalysisResultCount << ','
            << member.trainComplete << ',' << member.inferConfigured << ','
            << member.inferComplete << ',' << member.analyzeConfigured << ','
            << member.analyzeComplete << ','
            << RecommendationCampaignStatusConsistencyText(member.consistency);
        for (const auto& code : member.diagnosticCodes)
            out << ',' << code.size() << ':' << code;
    }
    return out.str();
}

RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus AggregateState(
    const RecommendationCampaignStatusSnapshot& result)
{
    const int count = static_cast<int>(result.members.size());
    if (result.inconsistentCount > 0)
        return RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus::inconsistent;
    if (result.executedCount == 0)
        return RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus::notExecuted;
    if (result.executedCount < count)
        return RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus::partiallyExecuted;
    if (result.activatedCount < count)
        return result.activatedCount == 0
            ? RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus::executedPaused
            : RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus::partiallyActivated;
    if (result.terminalCount == count)
    {
        if (result.completedCount == count)
            return RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus::completed;
        if (result.cancelledCount == count)
            return RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus::cancelled;
        if (result.failedCount > 0 && result.cancelledCount == 0)
            return RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus::completedWithFailures;
        return RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus::mixedTerminal;
    }
    if (result.runningCount > 0)
        return RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus::inProgress;
    if (result.pendingCount > 0)
        return RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus::queued;
    return RecommendationCampaignAggregateStatus::executedPaused;
}

} // namespace

RecommendationCampaignStatusRequest NormalizeRecommendationCampaignStatusRequest(
    const RecommendationCampaignStatusRequest& request)
{
    if (request.materializationId <= 0)
        throw std::invalid_argument("campaign_status_materialization_id_invalid");
    return request;
}

RecommendationCampaignStatusSnapshot BuildRecommendationCampaignStatusSnapshot(
    const RecommendationCampaignStatusRequest& request,
    RecommendationCampaignStatusInput input)
{
    RecommendationCampaignStatusSnapshot result;
    result.request = NormalizeRecommendationCampaignStatusRequest(request);
    if (input.materializationId != result.request.materializationId ||
        input.materializationContractVersion !=
            kRecommendationCampaignMaterializationContractVersion ||
        input.materializationIdentityCanonical.empty() ||
        input.materializationIdentityHash !=
            RecommendationCanonicalHash(input.materializationIdentityCanonical))
        throw std::invalid_argument("campaign_status_materialization_identity_invalid");
    if (input.selectedMemberCount <= 0 || input.members.empty())
        throw std::invalid_argument("campaign_status_zero_members");
    if (input.selectedMemberCount != static_cast<int>(input.members.size()))
        throw std::invalid_argument("campaign_status_member_count_mismatch");
    if (input.members.size() >
        static_cast<std::size_t>(kMaximumRecommendationCampaignStatusMembers))
        throw std::invalid_argument("campaign_status_member_limit_exceeded");
    if (input.observedAt.empty())
        throw std::invalid_argument("campaign_status_observed_at_required");

    std::sort(input.members.begin(), input.members.end(),
        [](const auto& left, const auto& right)
        {
            return left.memberOrdinal < right.memberOrdinal;
        });
    std::set<long long> memberIds;
    std::set<long long> proposalIds;
    std::set<long long> rankingIds;
    result.members.reserve(input.members.size());
    for (std::size_t index = 0; index < input.members.size(); ++index)
    {
        const auto& member = input.members[index];
        if (member.memberOrdinal != static_cast<int>(index) + 1 ||
            member.materializationMemberId <= 0 || member.recommendationId <= 0 ||
            member.sourceExperimentId <= 0 || member.rankingMemberId <= 0 ||
            member.proposalId <= 0 ||
            !memberIds.insert(member.materializationMemberId).second ||
            !proposalIds.insert(member.proposalId).second ||
Phase 5 Step 4 — Recommendation Campaign Operational Status
=============================================================

Purpose and authority
---------------------

Phase 5 Step 4 is a read-only point-in-time projection for one persisted Phase
4D campaign materialization. It reports every exact member's ordinary Phase 4C
review, execution, activation, experiment lifecycle, worker metadata, model,
final inference, and final analysis evidence. It creates no campaign status or
lifecycle authority and persists nothing.

The immutable ordered rows in
``experiment_recommendation_campaign_materialization_member`` are the only
membership source. The production Phase 4D loader validates the manifest,
canonical identity and hash, selected count, contiguous ordinals, unique links,
and exact proposal provenance before downstream status is projected.

Snapshot and query contract
---------------------------

One PostgreSQL ``REPEATABLE READ``, ``READ ONLY`` transaction owns the complete
snapshot. The operation takes no advisory or row locks, performs no write or
sequence call, and uses bounded exact-ID queries for the materialized proposals
and their linked experiments. Unrelated workflow and experiment rows cannot
become campaign members.

``observed_at`` is one PostgreSQL transaction timestamp shared by every output
row. It is metadata, not identity. The deterministic version-1 snapshot
identity binds the validated materialization and every ordered member's durable
review, execution, activation, experiment lifecycle, worker, model, phase-
completion, exact completed/failed final-result counts, consistency, and
diagnostic projection.
Persisted lifecycle timestamps are evidence and are bound; ``observed_at`` and
local wall-clock time are not identity inputs. The identity contains no random
value, local command process ID, transaction ID, or unordered iteration. A
persisted ``worker_pid`` is bound as database evidence but is not treated as a
live-process observation.

Member and aggregate status
---------------------------

Execution, activation, operational state, terminal result, and consistency are
separate dimensions. Operational states distinguish proposal-only, executed
paused, pending scheduler claim, running train/infer/analyze, completed, failed,
cancelled, paused after progress, and inconsistent evidence. An execution keeps
its immutable original authorizing approval even when the greatest current
review-decision ID later records rejection.

Final inference is configured only when both persisted inference dates exist.
For ordinary recommendation-conversion experiments, the existing scheduler
requires that range after training and advances completed final inference to
final analysis. Successful ``completed/done`` therefore requires the exact
final inference row selected by the scheduler (current model, symbol, horizon,
threshold, and dates) and completed final analysis for the current model. A
persisted ``completed/done`` recommendation-conversion experiment without that
configured pipeline is reported as inconsistent rather than treated as a
train-only success.

Unknown lifecycle values, invalid cardinality or provenance, activation without
execution, missing experiments, incompatible activation/lifecycle state,
incoherent worker evidence, and missing configured terminal results remain
visible as deterministic member diagnostics. A valid materialization still
returns every ordered member when downstream evidence is malformed. An invalid
materialization rejects the command because exact membership cannot be trusted.
Any inconsistent member forces aggregate ``inconsistent`` status.

CLI and output
--------------

::

   LSTM_Release --recommendation-campaign-status=MATERIALIZATION_ID
   LSTM_Release --recommendation-campaign-status MATERIALIZATION_ID

The positive ID is required. Duplicate options, ``--dry-run``, ``--yes``, and
combinations with any other standalone operation are rejected before database
dispatch because the command is intrinsically read-only.

``RECOMMENDATION_CAMPAIGN_STATUS`` reports snapshot/materialization identities,
aggregate state and counts, percentages and epoch aggregates where meaningful,
and explicit no-write/no-lock/no-scheduler safety fields. It is followed by one
``RECOMMENDATION_CAMPAIGN_STATUS_MEMBER`` record per persisted ordinal with
exact provenance IDs, independent state dimensions, persisted lifecycle and
worker fields, phase-completion flags, consistency, and diagnostics. Optional
values use ``null`` and variable text uses the production percent encoder.

The snapshot is advisory and may become stale immediately after its transaction
ends. It observes persisted scheduler/worker evidence only; it does not inspect
processes, poll or signal the scheduler, launch workers, or control experiments.

Schema and boundaries
---------------------

No migration or privilege change is required. Runtime access uses existing
``SELECT`` privileges. Step 1 remains execute-only, Step 2 activate-only, Step 3
atomic execute-and-activate, and Step 4 observational only. Step 4 invokes none
of the preceding mutation commands and performs no automatic follow-up.

References
----------

* ``docs/Phase4DExperimentRecommendationCampaignHandoff.rst``
* ``docs/Phase5ExperimentRecommendationCampaignExecution.rst``
* ``docs/Phase5ExperimentRecommendationCampaignActivation.rst``
* ``docs/Phase5ExperimentRecommendationCampaignLaunch.rst``
* ``docs/architecture/Volume_VIII_Recommendation_Engine.md``

This is the first implementation increment of the Recommendation Campaign Outcome Assessment feature.

Repository state

Expected branch:
phase5

Expected worktree:
clean

Begin by verifying:

git branch --show-current
git rev-parse HEAD
git status --short
git diff --check
git diff --cached --check

If the repository is not clean, stop and report why.

Do not fetch.
Do not pull.
Do not merge.
Do not rebase.
Do not switch branches.

Purpose

Implement only the pure domain contract and deterministic assessment model.

Do NOT implement:

- PostgreSQL repository access
- CLI parsing
- scheduler integration
- worker interaction
- service layer
- persistence
- documentation beyond comments required for the new types

Architecture

This feature is a read-only assessment.

It compares:

frozen recommendation evidence

↓

exact materialized experiment

↓

final experiment analysis evidence

↓

deterministic comparison

It does NOT:

- evaluate recommendation policy
- determine whether the recommendation was scientifically good
- decide whether another experiment should be run
- score campaigns
- modify database state
- communicate with the scheduler
- infer process liveness
- persist assessment results

Terminology

Use the term:

Recommendation Campaign Outcome Assessment

Do not use simply "Outcome" for externally visible names.

Future CLI (not implemented in this step) will be:

--recommendation-campaign-outcome-assessment

Domain requirements

Design immutable domain structures representing:

assessment identity

campaign identity

materialization identity

member identity

source evidence

result evidence

comparison context

comparison classification

assessment summary

member assessment

Define explicit comparison classifications including at minimum:

Comparable

ContextChanged

MissingSourceEvidence

MissingResultEvidence

ResultNotTerminal

MetricUnavailable

UnsupportedComparison

Requirements

Every comparison must be deterministic.

A comparison is Comparable only if all scientifically relevant context matches.

At minimum compare:

symbol

prediction horizon

threshold

window size

label definition

metric identity

inference range

If any differ:

classification becomes ContextChanged

no numeric delta is produced

Delta values are optional.

Never compute a delta when contexts differ.

Metric identities

Metrics are identified by both:

metric name

metric value

Never compare metrics solely because both contain floating-point values.

Example:

infer_accuracy

leader_score

validation_accuracy

must never be mixed.

Assessment semantics

Assessment is:

read-only

point-in-time

non-persistent

non-authoritative

The assessment does not declare a campaign successful or unsuccessful.

It reports only evidence.

Testing

Add comprehensive pure unit tests covering:

identical contexts

different symbols

different horizons

different thresholds

different metric names

missing source evidence

missing result evidence

non-terminal result

unsupported comparisons

deterministic equality

deterministic ordering

null delta generation

context mismatch

No PostgreSQL tests.

No scheduler tests.

No CLI tests.

No integration tests.

No filesystem access.

Constraints

No database schema changes.

No repository changes.

No services.

No CLI.

No scheduler.

No process inspection.

No documentation edits except comments directly associated with new types.

Deliverables

Implement:

pure domain types

comparison rules

comparison classification

deterministic assessment model

CURRENT IMPLEMENTED CAMPAIGN PATH

Phase 4D provides:

1. deterministic read-only campaign planning;
2. deterministic read-only campaign review;
3. explicit persisted approval/rejection;
4. exact approved-campaign materialization into Phase 4C proposals;
5. read-only campaign handoff;
6. explicit atomic campaign-wide Phase 4C proposal review.

Phase 5 provides:

1. atomic execution of exact materialized members into paused experiments;
2. atomic activation of exact executed members into pending/train;
3. atomic execution-and-activation launch;
4. repeatable-read, read-only operational status across exact campaign membership and downstream experiment/result evidence.

The existing scheduler independently processes ordinary pending experiments.

AUTHORITATIVE SOURCES TO INSPECT

At minimum inspect:

docs/Phase4DExperimentRecommendationCampaignHandoff.rst
docs/Phase5ExperimentRecommendationCampaignExecution.rst
docs/Phase5ExperimentRecommendationCampaignActivation.rst
docs/Phase5ExperimentRecommendationCampaignLaunch.rst
docs/Phase5ExperimentRecommendationCampaignStatus.rst
docs/architecture/Volume_VIII_Recommendation_Engine.md

Relevant production and test code for:

- campaign materialization;
- campaign review;
- campaign execution;
- campaign activation;
- campaign launch;
- campaign operational status;
- Phase 4C conversion workflow;
- scheduler experiment lifecycle and operational controls;
- continuation automation;
- recommendation/campaign CLI dispatch and conflict validation.

GOAL

Recommend exactly one next Phase 5 step that provides real operational or research value and forms a coherent, bounded extension of Steps 1–4.

Do not assume that “Step 5” must be autonomous campaign management.

Evaluate candidate directions such as:

1. explicit campaign-wide pause, resume, cancel, retry, or stop controls;
2. an explicit campaign reconciliation or repair command;
3. persisted campaign lifecycle authority;
4. scheduler-aware campaign monitoring or admission control;
5. bounded automatic campaign progression;
6. campaign completion/failure summary persistence;
7. no additional Phase 5 step yet, with a recommendation to move to another project phase.

These are candidates, not requirements.

ARCHITECTURAL CONSTRAINTS

Preserve existing authority boundaries unless the selected step explicitly and convincingly justifies changing one.

Do not casually introduce:

- hidden automation;
- background scans;
- automatic proposal review;
- automatic campaign approval;
- automatic experiment creation outside existing Phase 4C primitives;
- a second experiment lifecycle authority;
- duplicated scheduler state;
- mutable campaign membership;
- profitability claims;
- score-based authorization;
- process-liveness inference from persisted worker_pid;
- unbounded database scans;
- scheduler polling from read-only status code;
- broad locks over unrelated campaigns or experiments.

Any proposed mutation must be:

- explicit;
- separately confirmed where destructive or lifecycle-changing;
- transactionally bounded;
- idempotent or fail-closed;
- auditable;
- limited to exact immutable materialized membership;
- compatible with existing per-experiment operational controls;
- safe under partial progress and concurrent scheduler activity.

ANALYSIS REQUIRED

1. Reconstruct the exact current authority and data-flow boundaries.

2. Identify the most important operational gap remaining after Step 4.

3. Compare at least four plausible next-step candidates.

For each candidate report:

- user value;
- architectural fit;
- required schema changes;
- scheduler coupling;
- concurrency and locking risk;
- interaction with partially progressed campaigns;
- interaction with terminal, failed, cancelled, paused, and inconsistent members;
- idempotency/retry semantics;
- implementation size;
- test burden;
- risk of creating duplicate lifecycle authority;
- whether it should be Phase 5, deferred, or rejected.

4. Select one preferred next step.

The preferred step must have a narrow one-sentence purpose and a clear reason it is more valuable than the alternatives.

5. Define the complete proposed contract:

- command name and both accepted CLI forms;
- required IDs and options;
- confirmation behavior;
- dry-run behavior, if appropriate;
- exact membership authority;
- eligibility rules;
- per-member outcomes;
- aggregate outcomes;
- transaction boundaries;
- locking order;
- concurrency behavior;
- retry and duplicate behavior;
- failure and rollback semantics;
- scheduler interaction;
- worker interaction;
- process-liveness boundary;
- database schema/migration requirements;
- runtime privileges;
- machine-readable output;
- documentation changes;
- explicit non-goals.

6. Determine whether the step should be:

- one implementation step;
- split into multiple smaller steps;
- preceded by a schema-only or pure-domain step;
- postponed until additional experiment evidence exists.

7. Produce an implementation sequence with independently reviewable increments.

For every increment include:

- exact goal;
- likely files to add;
- likely files to modify;
- domain/repository/service/CLI boundaries;
- tests required;
- database safety requirements;
- regression suites required;
- completion criteria;
- proposed commit message.

8. Identify all unresolved questions. Resolve them from current code and documentation wherever possible. Only leave questions unresolved when the repository genuinely supplies no answer.

9. State whether implementation should begin now.

Do not recommend implementation merely to keep the scheduler busy. The next step must provide durable value.

REQUIRED FINAL RESPONSE

Use this structure:

## Repository state

## Current Phase 5 boundary

## Remaining operational gap

## Candidate comparison

Use a concise comparison table.

## Recommended Phase 5 Step 5

Include:

- title;
- one-sentence purpose;
- why this direction wins;
- why it belongs in Phase 5;
- why it is safe to implement now.

## Proposed contract

## Transaction and concurrency model

## Scheduler and worker boundary

## Schema and privilege impact

## CLI and machine output

## Test and regression plan

## Implementation increments

## Deferred and rejected alternatives

## Remaining risks and unresolved questions

## Recommendation

End with exactly one of:

“Proceed with Phase 5 Step 5 implementation planning.”

“Do not add Phase 5 Step 5 yet; move to <specific next activity>.”

After Step 4 reports that a campaign completed, an operator still cannot ask one bounded campaign command:

- which exact final inference and analysis rows belong to each member;
- how those results compare with the frozen source evidence that originally justified the recommendation;
- which parameter was changed and from what value to what value;
- which completed members lack usable metrics;
- which comparisons changed the evaluation context;
- whether unrelated experiments or historical models were excluded; or
- what deterministic campaign-level outcome identity represents that evidence.

The necessary source baseline already exists in recommendation rows—leader score, inference accuracy, predicted-neutral proportion, source experiment/model/analysis, and the mutation specification. Campaign planning already reads that frozen evidence. [The planning repository](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/Sources/ExperimentRecommendationCampaignPlanningRepository.cpp:143>) demonstrates its authoritative mapping.

Campaign-wide controls are valuable eventually, but they are not the safest next increment. Existing controls have action-specific semantics: pause accepts only pending, resume only paused, cancel rejects running, and retry accepts only failed. [Current control validation](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/Sources/ExperimentScheduler.cpp:4972>) confirms this. More importantly, the scheduler currently loads pending work and later performs an unconditional running-state update by experiment ID, creating an observe-then-update window that must be hardened before a campaign pause can safely race scheduler claims. [The launch-state update](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/Sources/ExperimentScheduler.cpp:11485>) does not condition on the row still being pending.

## Candidate comparison

| Candidate | User value and architectural fit | Schema and scheduler coupling | Partial/terminal behavior and concurrency | Retry, size, tests, duplicate authority | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Read-only campaign outcome assessment** | High research value; directly extends Step 4 using existing evidence and exact membership. | No schema; no scheduler coupling. | Repeatable-read snapshot, no locks; nonterminal, failed, cancelled, completed, and inconsistent members are reported separately. | Deterministic evidence hash; same evidence gives the same hash. Medium implementation/test burden. No lifecycle authority. | **Preferred Phase 5 Step 5.** |
| Campaign pause/resume/cancel/retry/stop | High operational value, but five materially different lifecycle actions do not form one narrow contract. | A safe, auditable design likely needs shared lifecycle primitives and operation audit; stop also couples to process supervision. | Pause/cancel race claims; running stop cannot be transactionally rolled back; mixed member states force either low-value all-or-nothing rejection or unsafe partial application. | Action-specific idempotency is currently absent. Large implementation and very high concurrency/process test burden. High duplicate-authority risk if implemented in campaign code. | Defer until lifecycle and scheduler claim hardening; split by action. |
| Reconciliation or repair | Useful only after a concrete diagnosed inconsistency. Step 4 already supplies reconciliation diagnostics. | Generic repair would need privileged mutation and repair audit; scheduler coupling varies by defect. | Immutable Phase 4C evidence cannot safely be “repaired” generically; partial/terminal rules are defect-specific. | No defensible generic retry identity. Large test matrix and high authority risk. | Reject generic repair; design only for a proven corruption class. |
| Persisted campaign lifecycle authority | Could coordinate operator intent and display a durable campaign state. | New state/event schema and eventual scheduler integration required. | Must reconcile every per-experiment transition, retry, cancellation, recovery, and continuation race. | Very large state-machine and migration burden. Directly duplicates Volume VII/XI authority. | Defer to controlled research automation, not Phase 5. |
| Scheduler-aware admission/capacity | Valuable for budgets, priority, or campaign concurrency limits. | Requires scheduler claim/query changes, schema/indexes, and an ADR under the accepted scheduler boundary. | Must arbitrate overlapping campaigns, unrelated pending work, capacity races, and recovery. | Very large scheduler regression burden; admission retry requires durable reservations. | Defer to Volume XI/Volume X design. |
| Bounded automatic progression | Convenience from review/materialization through launch or follow-up. | Requires durable requests/budgets and scheduler or background-loop integration. | Partial progress and failures require reconciliation; hidden scans risk repeated or unauthorized action. | Large implementation; request-ledger idempotency required. Risks second lifecycle authority and collapses explicit confirmations. | Reject for Phase 5; belongs in future Volume X work after ADRs. |
| Persisted terminal completion/failure summary | Strong eventual audit value. | New append-only snapshot/member schema; no direct scheduler coupling. | “Terminal” is not final because failed experiments can be retried and cancelled work may later require replacement. | Stop after Step 4 | Zero implementation risk. | None. | Leaves current member lifecycle untouched. | No tests or authority risk. | Rejected because the exact-result research gap can be closed safely without mutation. |

## Recommended Phase 5 Step 5

**Title:** Deterministic Recommendation Campaign Outcome Assessment

**One-sentence purpose:** Read one exact materialized campaign and deterministically compare its frozen recommendation baselines with its observed final inference and analysis outcomes, without mutating lifecycle state or making causal, authorization, or profitability claims.

**Why this direction wins:** It closes the recommendation-to-result research loop, uses evidence already produced by the system, and avoids the unresolved scheduler, process, audit, and finality problems attached to control or persistence proposals.

**Why it belongs in Phase 5:** Phase 5 already owns explicit campaign execution, activation, launch, and operational observation; assessing the resulting exact experiments is the bounded research-facing completion of that path.

**Why it is safe now:** It can reuse Step 4 membership/lifecycle validation and perform additional exact-ID reads in the same repeatable-read transaction. It needs no schema, no live process information, no scheduler changes, and no additional experiment evidence to define or test the contract.

## Proposed contract

### Command

Both forms are accepted:

```text
LSTM_Release --recommendation-campaign-outcome=MATERIALIZATION_ID
LSTM_Release --recommendation-campaign-outcome MATERIALIZATION_ID
```

Contract rules:

- One positive materialization ID is required.
- Duplicate forms and combinations with any other standalone operation are rejected before database dispatch.
- There are no filters and no list command in Step 5; this prevents broad scans.
- `--yes` is rejected because the command is read-only.
- `--dry-run` is rejected because the command is intrinsically a dry read.
- No operator, reason, score threshold, or profitability option is accepted.

### Membership authority

Membership is exactly the stored one-based ordinal sequence in:

- `experiment_recommendation_campaign_materialization`; and
- `experiment_recommendation_campaign_materialization_member`.

The existing materialization validator must establish:

- supported contract version;
- valid canonical/hash pair;
- positive member count of at most 1000;
- count agreement;
- contiguous ordinals;
- unique member, ranking-member, and proposal links; and
- exact recommendation, source-experiment, and proposal provenance.

Continuation children, replacement experiments, semantic duplicates, current ranking output, and inferred proposal matches are excluded.

### Eligibility and evidence rules

The inspection command is valid at any campaign progress state. Research metrics are available for a member only when:

1. Step 4 classifies its downstream evidence as consistent;
2. it is terminal `completed/done`;
3. it has exactly one current model linked to the experiment;
4. it has exactly one completed final inference row matching current model, symbol, horizon, threshold, inference dates, final scope, and null checkpoint linkage;
5. it has exactly one completed final analysis row matching experiment, current model, and final scope;
6. the materialized recommendation and proposal still match their stored provenance; and
7. required numeric values are finite.

The frozen source baseline comes from the persisted recommendation, not by reopening and recomputing the source experiment:

- source experiment/model/analysis IDs;
- source leader score;
- source inference accuracy;
- optional source predicted-neutral proportion;
- source evidence count;
- changed parameter;
- source and proposed canonical values; and
- source/proposed invocation identities.

The observed result side reports:

- campaign experiment and model IDs;
- exact final inference-result ID and accuracy;
- exact final analysis ID;
- leader score;
- inference accuracy;
- derived predicted-neutral proportion and evidence count; and
- result timestamps and identity/provenance fields.

### Comparison context

Each successful member is classified as:

- `same_evaluation_context` when source and proposed symbol, horizon, label threshold, and inference date range match; or
- `changed_evaluation_context` otherwise.

Raw source and outcome metrics remain visible in both cases. Leader-score and inference-accuracy delta classifications are included in campaign aggregates only for `same_evaluation_context`; Step 5 does not pretend cross-horizon or cross-threshold values are directly comparable.

### Per-member outcomes

Each member has exactly one outcome:

- `not_ready`: proposal-only, paused, pending, running, or paused after progress;
- `succeeded_comparable`: completed with complete metrics and the same evaluation context;
- `succeeded_context_changed`: completed with metrics but changed evaluation context;
- `succeeded_metric_gap`: valid completed lifecycle/result rows but leader score or inference accuracy is unavailable;
- `terminal_failed`: valid failed lifecycle;
- `terminal_cancelled`: valid cancelled lifecycle; or
- `inconsistent`: invalid membership-adjacent or downstream provenance/cardinality/lifecycle/result evidence.

A failed or cancelled member is an observed research outcome, not an integrity error.

### Aggregate outcome precedence

1. Any inconsistent member: `inconsistent`.
2. Any nonterminal member: `not_ready`.
3. All cancelled: `cancelled`.
4. All succeeded with complete metrics: `completed`.
5. All succeeded but at least one metric gap: `completed_with_metric_gaps`.
6. All terminal, with at least one failure and no cancellation: `completed_with_failures`.
7. Any other all-terminal mixture: `mixed_terminal`.

The aggregate also reports comparable/context-changed/metric-gap counts and positive/zero/negative delta counts for leader score and inference accuracy. It does not compute a campaign score, weighted mean, statistical significance, profitability, or authorization decision.

### Identity and retry contract

Outcome contract version 1 canonically binds:

- materialization ID, version, identity, and ordered membership;
- the Step 4 status snapshot identity;
- frozen recommendation source evidence;
- proposal mutation and invocation evidence;
- exact result IDs, statuses, timestamps, and metric values;
- per-member outcome, comparison context, and diagnostics; and
- aggregate counts.

`observed_at`, local time, process ID, transaction ID, and row-return order are excluded.

An exact retry over unchanged durable evidence produces the same outcome identity hash. If scheduler state or result evidence changes, the command produces a new point-in-time identity. No duplicate row can be created because nothing is persisted.

### Explicit non-goals

Step 5 does not:

- approve, reject, execute, activate, pause, resume, cancel, retry, stop, or repair anything;
- persist a completion or outcome row;
- close a campaign or prevent later experiment retries;
- follow continuation or lineage children;
- query process tables or infer liveness;
- poll, signal, start, stop, or configure the scheduler;
- create a profitability or causal-improvement claim;
- use outcome metrics as authorization;
- scan unrelated campaigns or experiments; or
- automatically invoke any earlier or later command.

## Transaction and concurrency model

One PostgreSQL `READ ONLY`, `REPEATABLE READ` transaction owns the complete operation.

The repository should:

1. load and validate the materialization;
2. build the existing Step 4 status snapshot inside the same transaction;
3. load recommendation and proposal evidence using exact member IDs;
4. load exact final inference rows using the bounded experiment/model/configuration set;
5. load exact final analysis rows using the bounded experiment/model set; and
6. build the pure outcome assessment.

The query count must be fixed with respect to member count—no per-member database loop. All `IN`/`VALUES` inputs are bounded by 1000 exact immutable members.

Locking order is **none**:

- no advisory locks;
- no row locks;
- no table locks requested by application code.

A concurrent scheduler transition or result update is either visible throughout the snapshot or invisible throughout it. The command never returns a hybrid of before/after evidence. It may become stale immediately after the transaction ends.

Invalid materialization evidence prevents a trusted membership set and fails the command. Downstream inconsistency remains observable for every exact member and does not trigger repair. Any exception ends the read transaction with no state to roll back.

## Scheduler and worker boundary

The command reads persisted experiment and result evidence only.

It does not:

- read scheduler process lists;
- inspect `/proc`, `ps`, or child-process state;
- signal any PID;
- trust `worker_pid` as proof of liveness;
- reserve capacity;
- alter polling eligibility;
- create claims; or
- update experiment state.

This preserves ADR-0004, which requires new scheduler-managed behavior to define eligibility, capacity, recovery, idempotency, and observability explicitly. [ADR-0004](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/architecture/adr/ADR-0004-scheduler-ownership-boundaries.md:21>) also forbids trusting PID evidence alone.

Campaign lifecycle controls should remain deferred until scheduler claim updates are conditional/atomic and lifecycle mutations are exposed through shared audited primitives. Running stop is especially unsuitable for campaign atomicity because the existing implementation signals processes one at a time and persists each cancellation separately. [Current stop behavior](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/Sources/ExperimentScheduler.cpp:14248>) is intentionally best-effort, not rollback-capable.

## Schema and privilege impact

- No migration.
- No new table, sequence, index, view, trigger, function, or campaign state.
- No new runtime grant.
- The command uses existing `SELECT` capability for materialization, recommendations, proposals, workflow evidence, experiments, models, final inference results, and final analysis results.
- No sequence is read for allocation or advanced.
- Existing result rows remain their current mutable operational records. The outcome identity is therefore a point-in-time assessment, not immutable result history.

A schema-presence check should fail clearly if required existing columns or final-result scope migrations are missing.

## CLI and machine output

Successful output begins with:

```text
RECOMMENDATION_CAMPAIGN_OUTCOME
```

followed by one row per exact ordinal:

```text
RECOMMENDATION_CAMPAIGN_OUTCOME_MEMBER
```

Failure emits:

```text
RECOMMENDATION_CAMPAIGN_OUTCOME_FAILED
```

The summary should include:

- materialization, status-snapshot, and outcome identities;
- `observed_at`;
- aggregate outcome and Step 4 operational status;
- terminal, nonterminal, completed, failed, cancelled, inconsistent, comparable, context-changed, and metric-gap counts;
- delta-sign counts;
- deterministic diagnostics; and
- explicit safety fields.

Member rows should include:

- all immutable membership/provenance IDs;
- operational and terminal state;
- mutation parameter and canonical source/proposed values;
- source experiment/model/analysis evidence and metrics;
- result experiment/model/inference/analysis IDs and metrics;
- comparison context and deltas;
- outcome state, consistency, and diagnostics.

Optional values use `null`; variable text uses the existing machine percent encoder. Every row should state:

```text
transaction_read_only=true
snapshot_isolation=repeatable_read
advisory_locks_acquired=0
row_locks_acquired=0
rows_inserted=0
rows_updated=0
rows_deleted=0
sequences_advanced=0
scheduler_polled=false
scheduler_signaled=false
worker_processes_inspected=false
workers_signaled=false
profitability_evaluated=false
authorization_granted=false
automatic_follow_up=false
```

  - request normalization and 1000-member bound;
  - ordinal and ID alignment;
  - every per-member state;
  - aggregate precedence;
  - source/proposed comparison-context classification;
  - null, non-finite, mismatched, and contradictory metrics;
  - deterministic canonical identity under shuffled input;
  - `observed_at` exclusion;
  - metric/result/status changes altering identity;
  - exact zero/positive/negative delta classification.

- **Repository tests on an explicit disposable non-`LSTM` database**
- **Repository tests on an explicit disposable non-`LSTM` database**
  - exact materialization membership only;
  - unrelated campaign, experiment, model, inference, and analysis rows ignored;
  - exact final-inference identity selection;
  - current-model final-analysis selection;
  - historical models and checkpoint results ignored;
  - missing, duplicate, failed, or wrong-provenance result diagnostics;
  - failed, cancelled, partially progressed, and mixed-terminal campaigns;
  - repeatable-read stability across concurrent updates;
  - changed snapshot after a later transaction;
  - runtime read-only behavior;
  - unchanged table digests, `updated_at` values, and sequence state;

- **Service and CLI tests**

## Implementation increments

This should be one logical Phase 5 step delivered as three reviewable commits. It needs no schema-only increment, should begin with a pure-domain increment, and should not be postponed for live experiment evidence.

### Increment 1 — Pure outcome contract

- **Goal:** Define outcome inputs, states, diagnostics, comparison-context rules, aggregate precedence, and deterministic identity without PostgreSQL.
- **Add:**
  - `Sources/ExperimentRecommendationCampaignOutcome.hpp`
  - `Sources/ExperimentRecommendationCampaignOutcome.cpp`
  - `Tests/ExperimentRecommendationCampaignOutcomeTests.cpp`
- **Modify:**
  - `ExpertAdvisor.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj`
- **Boundaries:** Domain normalization and derivation only; no repository, CLI, process, or lifecycle code.
- **Tests:** Complete pure state matrix, ordering, identity, metric validation, and aggregate tests.
- **Database safety:** No database dependency or `pqxx` include.
- **Regression:** Existing Step 4 pure tests.
- **Completion:** Every valid input has one deterministic member and aggregate outcome; malformed membership/evidence fails closed.
- **Commit:** `Phase 5 Step 5a: Define campaign outcome assessment contract`

### Increment 2 — Bounded read-only repository

- **Goal:** Load Step 4 status plus frozen recommendation, proposal, and exact final-result evidence in one repeatable-read transaction.
- **Add:**

  - `Sources/ExperimentRecommendationCampaignOutcomeRepository.cpp`
  - `Tests/ExperimentRecommendationCampaignOutcomeRepositoryTests.cpp`
- **Modify:**

  - Only if needed for behavior-preserving reuse: `Sources/ExperimentRecommendationCampaignStatusRepository.hpp/.cpp`
- **Boundaries:** Repository owns schema checks, exact-ID queries, row mapping, and transaction wrapper; domain owns classification and identity.
- **Tests:** Disposable-database provenance, boundedness, concurrency snapshot, result-cardinality, no-write, no-lock, and runtime-access tests.
- **Database safety:** Refuse database name `LSTM`; use a per-process schema; apply existing migrations only; verify row/sequence/timestamp digests unchanged.
- **Regression:** Step 4 status repository, materialization, handoff, workflow, and recommendation persistence suites.
- **Completion:** Fixed query count, exact 1000-member maximum, stable snapshot under concurrent writes, and no DML/sequence activity.
- **Commit:** `Phase 5 Step 5b: Add read-only campaign outcome repository`

### Increment 3 — CLI, machine output, and architecture documentation

- **Goal:** Expose both CLI forms, standalone conflict validation, stable machine records, and the complete safety contract.
- **Add:**

- **Tests:** Service formatting, both release CLI forms, conflicts, invalid options, and end-to-end read-only output.
- **Database safety:** Same disposable-database guard; no migration; verify no state changes after both CLI forms.
- **Regression:** All Phase 5 suites plus CLI dispatch and scheduler child-status tests.
- **Completion:** Documentation, help, parser, service, and repository agree exactly; output explicitly denies mutation, scheduler, worker, profitability, and authorization effects.
- **Commit:** `Phase 5 Step 5: Add recommendation campaign outcome assessment`

## Deferred and rejected alternatives

- Campaign controls should be deferred and later designed as separate pause, resume, cancel, retry, and stop capabilities—not one omnibus Step 5. They first require shared per-experiment lifecycle primitives, durable audit semantics, conditional scheduler claims, and action-specific concurrency tests.
- Running campaign stop should not promise atomicity. Process signals are irreversible external effects and require a durable request plus compensating/reconciliation protocol.
- Generic repair is rejected. Step 4 already identifies inconsistencies; a repair command should be designed only for a specific reproduced corruption class.
- Persisted campaign lifecycle state, scheduler admission, budget reservations, and automatic progression belong to future Volume X/XI architecture and require ADRs.
- An immutable outcome-snapshot table is deferred until retry/replacement/finality semantics are decided and the read-only outcome schema has been exercised.
- Moving immediately to another phase would leave a safe, high-value research gap unresolved.

## Remaining risks and unresolved questions

Resolved from current code and documentation:

- Membership authority is the immutable materialization, not experiments or rankings.
- Frozen source metrics come from persisted recommendation evidence.
- Final results are selected using the current campaign experiment model and the scheduler’s exact final inference/final analysis identity.
- Failed and cancelled members are valid terminal outcomes.

exec
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '21290,21360p' Phase5Step5ArchitecturePlanningOutput.txt" in /Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor
 succeeded in 0ms:
- Failed and cancelled members are valid terminal outcomes.
- `worker_pid` is persisted evidence only.
- Continuation descendants are outside campaign membership.
- No profitability evidence exists, so no profitability conclusion is permitted.

Genuinely unresolved—and intentionally excluded from Step 5:

1. **Outcome finality:** failed experiments are retryable and result rows are operationally mutable/upserted. The repository supplies no authoritative “campaign closed forever” event. Step 5 must therefore remain a point-in-time assessment.
2. **Scientific aggregation policy:** the repository defines no accepted weighting, materiality threshold, significance test, or rule for cross-horizon/cross-threshold comparisons. Step 5 should expose raw metrics, explicit context, and conservative delta counts only.
3. **Safe bulk lifecycle mutation:** scheduler claim hardening and a shared audited lifecycle service do not yet exist. This blocks a safe campaign pause/resume/cancel/retry design.
4. **Historical snapshot retention:** there is no decision on whether outcome snapshots should be unique per materialization, append for every changed evidence identity, or require explicit campaign closure. Persistence should wait for that decision.

Implementation should begin now with the pure-domain increment; it does not need a migration or live campaign evidence.

## Recommendation

Proceed with Phase 5 Step 5 implementation planning.

## Current Phase 5 boundary

The implemented authority and data flow is:

```text
immutable ranking snapshot
  → read-only campaign plan
  → read-only campaign review
  → immutable explicit campaign approval/rejection
  → immutable materialization manifest and ordered members
  → ordinary Phase 4C proposal reviews
  → ordinary Phase 4C executions creating paused experiments
  → ordinary Phase 4C activations making experiments pending
  → existing scheduler lifecycle
  → final inference and analysis evidence
  → read-only Phase 5 operational status
```

The exact boundaries are:

- The materialization manifest and ordered member rows are the sole campaign-membership authority. Rankings, policies, recommendations, current queries, experiment lineage, and continuation descendants cannot add members.
- Current Phase 4C review authority remains the greatest review-decision ID for each proposal.
- Phase 4C execution rows remain the durable authority linking proposals and approving reviews to paused experiments.
- Phase 4C activation rows remain the durable authority for the exact `paused/train → pending/train` transition.
- Phase 5 Steps 1–3 are atomic conveniences over those existing authorities. They introduce no campaign execution, activation, launch, or lifecycle table. [Step 3’s contract](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/Phase5ExperimentRecommendationCampaignLaunch.rst:7>) explicitly leaves durable authority in materialization, Phase 4C, and experiment rows.
- The scheduler sees ordinary `experiment` rows only. It does not consume campaign, proposal, review, execution, or activation tables.
- The scheduler owns claims, process launch, completion, recovery, and capacity. Recommendation and campaign code must not become a second scheduler.
- Step 4 reads one repeatable-read snapshot, takes no locks, and treats `worker_pid` only as persisted evidence—not process-liveness proof. [The status contract](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/Phase5ExperimentRecommendationCampaignStatus.rst:7>) establishes these rules.
- Step 4 classifies terminal state and validates the presence/cardinality of final results, but its public model contains only final-result counts and completion flags—not result IDs or research metrics. [The current status model](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/Sources/ExperimentRecommendationCampaignStatus.hpp:79>) stops at those counts.
- Continuation automation remains independent. Any continuation child is a new ordinary experiment and is not silently added to immutable materialization membership.

## Remaining operational gap

The most important remaining gap is not campaign execution control; it is the missing research outcome projection.

After Step 4 reports that a campaign completed, an operator still cannot ask one bounded campaign command:

   407	RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentComparison::
   408	    RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentComparison(
   409	        RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentComparisonClassification
   410	            classificationValue,
   411	        std::optional<double> deltaValue,
   412	        std::vector<RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentContextDifference>
   413	            contextDifferencesValue)
   414	    : classification(classificationValue),
   415	      delta(deltaValue),
   416	      contextDifferences(std::move(contextDifferencesValue))
   417	{
   418	    if (classification ==
   419	            RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentComparisonClassification::
   420	                Comparable)
   421	    {
   422	        if (!delta || !std::isfinite(*delta) || !contextDifferences.empty())
   423	            throw std::invalid_argument(
   424	                "recommendation_campaign_outcome_assessment_comparable_invalid");
   425	    }
   426	    else if (delta)
   427	        throw std::invalid_argument(
   428	            "recommendation_campaign_outcome_assessment_noncomparable_delta_invalid");
   429	    if (classification ==
   430	            RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentComparisonClassification::
   431	                ContextChanged &&
   432	        contextDifferences.empty())
   433	        throw std::invalid_argument(
   434	            "recommendation_campaign_outcome_assessment_context_change_missing");
   435	    if (classification !=
   436	            RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentComparisonClassification::
   437	                ContextChanged &&
   438	        !contextDifferences.empty())
   439	        throw std::invalid_argument(
   440	            "recommendation_campaign_outcome_assessment_context_change_unexpected");
   441	}

   443	RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentSummary::
   444	    RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentSummary(
   445	        int memberCountValue,
   446	        int comparableCountValue,
   447	        int contextChangedCountValue,
   448	        int missingSourceEvidenceCountValue,
   449	        int missingResultEvidenceCountValue,
   450	        int resultNotTerminalCountValue,
   451	        int metricUnavailableCountValue,
   452	        int unsupportedComparisonCountValue,
   453	        int positiveDeltaCountValue,
   454	        int zeroDeltaCountValue,
   455	        int negativeDeltaCountValue)
   456	    : memberCount(memberCountValue),
   457	      comparableCount(comparableCountValue),
   458	      contextChangedCount(contextChangedCountValue),
   459	      missingSourceEvidenceCount(missingSourceEvidenceCountValue),
   460	      missingResultEvidenceCount(missingResultEvidenceCountValue),
   461	      resultNotTerminalCount(resultNotTerminalCountValue),
   462	      metricUnavailableCount(metricUnavailableCountValue),
   463	      unsupportedComparisonCount(unsupportedComparisonCountValue),
   464	      positiveDeltaCount(positiveDeltaCountValue),
   465	      zeroDeltaCount(zeroDeltaCountValue),
   466	      negativeDeltaCount(negativeDeltaCountValue)
   467	{
   468	    const int classifiedCount = comparableCount + contextChangedCount +
   469	        missingSourceEvidenceCount + missingResultEvidenceCount +
   470	        resultNotTerminalCount + metricUnavailableCount +
   471	        unsupportedComparisonCount;
   472	    const int deltaCount =
   473	        positiveDeltaCount + zeroDeltaCount + negativeDeltaCount;
   474	    if (memberCount < 0 || comparableCount < 0 || contextChangedCount < 0 ||
   475	        missingSourceEvidenceCount < 0 || missingResultEvidenceCount < 0 ||
   476	        resultNotTerminalCount < 0 || metricUnavailableCount < 0 ||
   477	        unsupportedComparisonCount < 0 || positiveDeltaCount < 0 ||
   478	        zeroDeltaCount < 0 || negativeDeltaCount < 0 ||
   479	        classifiedCount != memberCount || deltaCount != comparableCount)
   480	        throw std::invalid_argument(
   481	            "recommendation_campaign_outcome_assessment_summary_invalid");
   482	}

   539	
   540	RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentComparison
   541	CompareRecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentEvidence(
   542	    const std::optional<RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentSourceEvidence>&
   543	        sourceEvidence,
   544	    const std::optional<RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentResultEvidence>&
   545	        resultEvidence)
   546	{
   547	    using Classification =
   548	        RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentComparisonClassification;
   549	    using Difference =
   550	        RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentContextDifference;
   551	    if (!sourceEvidence)
   552	        return {Classification::MissingSourceEvidence, std::nullopt, {}};
   553	    if (!resultEvidence)
   554	        return {Classification::MissingResultEvidence, std::nullopt, {}};
   555	    if (resultEvidence->state ==
   556	        RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentResultState::NotTerminal)
   557	        return {Classification::ResultNotTerminal, std::nullopt, {}};
   558	
   559	    std::vector<Difference> differences;
   560	    if (sourceEvidence->context.symbol != resultEvidence->context.symbol)
   561	        differences.push_back(Difference::Symbol);
   562	    if (sourceEvidence->context.predictionHorizon !=
   563	        resultEvidence->context.predictionHorizon)
   564	        differences.push_back(Difference::PredictionHorizon);
   565	    if (sourceEvidence->context.threshold != resultEvidence->context.threshold)
   566	        differences.push_back(Difference::Threshold);
   567	    if (sourceEvidence->context.windowSize !=
   568	        resultEvidence->context.windowSize)
   569	        differences.push_back(Difference::WindowSize);
   570	    if (sourceEvidence->context.labelDefinition !=
   571	        resultEvidence->context.labelDefinition)
   572	        differences.push_back(Difference::LabelDefinition);
   573	    if (sourceEvidence->metric.name != resultEvidence->metric.name)
   574	        differences.push_back(Difference::MetricIdentity);
   575	    if (sourceEvidence->context.inferenceRangeStart !=
   576	            resultEvidence->context.inferenceRangeStart ||
   577	        sourceEvidence->context.inferenceRangeEnd !=
   578	            resultEvidence->context.inferenceRangeEnd)
   579	        differences.push_back(Difference::InferenceRange);
   580	    if (!differences.empty())
   581	        return {Classification::ContextChanged, std::nullopt,
   582	            std::move(differences)};
   583	
   584	    if (!sourceEvidence->metric.value || !resultEvidence->metric.value)
   585	        return {Classification::MetricUnavailable, std::nullopt, {}};
   586	    if (sourceEvidence->metric.comparisonSupport !=
   587	            RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentMetricComparisonSupport::
   588	                NumericDelta ||
   589	        resultEvidence->metric.comparisonSupport !=
   590	            RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentMetricComparisonSupport::
   591	                NumericDelta)
   592	        return {Classification::UnsupportedComparison, std::nullopt, {}};
   593	
   594	    double delta =
   595	        *resultEvidence->metric.value - *sourceEvidence->metric.value;
   596	    if (!std::isfinite(delta))
   597	        return {Classification::UnsupportedComparison, std::nullopt, {}};
   598	    if (delta == 0.0) delta = 0.0;
   599	    return {Classification::Comparable, delta, {}};
   600	}
   601	
   602	RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessment BuildRecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessment(
   603	    const RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentCampaignIdentity&
   604	        campaignIdentity,
   605	    const RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentMaterializationIdentity&
   606	        materializationIdentity,
   607	    const std::string& observedAt,
   608	    const std::vector<RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentMemberEvidence>&
   609	        members)
   610	{
   611	    RequireText(observedAt,
   612	        "recommendation_campaign_outcome_assessment_observed_at_invalid");
   613	    if (materializationIdentity.campaignApprovalId !=
   614	        campaignIdentity.campaignApprovalId)
   615	        throw std::invalid_argument(
   616	            "recommendation_campaign_outcome_assessment_campaign_materialization_mismatch");
   617	    if (materializationIdentity.contractVersion !=
   618	        kRecommendationCampaignMaterializationContractVersion)
   619	        throw std::invalid_argument(
   620	            "recommendation_campaign_outcome_assessment_materialization_version_unsupported");
   621	    if (materializationIdentity.memberCount !=
   622	        static_cast<int>(members.size()))
   623	        throw std::invalid_argument(
   624	            "recommendation_campaign_outcome_assessment_member_count_mismatch");
   625	    if (members.empty() ||
   626	        members.size() > static_cast<std::size_t>(
   627	                             kMaximumRecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentMembers))
   628	        throw std::invalid_argument(
   629	            "recommendation_campaign_outcome_assessment_member_count_invalid");
   630	
   631	    std::vector<const RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentMemberEvidence*>
   632	        ordered;
   633	    ordered.reserve(members.size());
   634	    for (const auto& member : members) ordered.push_back(&member);
   635	    std::sort(ordered.begin(), ordered.end(), [](const auto* left, const auto* right)
   636	    {
   637	        return std::tie(left->identity.memberOrdinal,
   638	                   left->identity.materializationMemberId,
   639	                   left->identity.rankingMemberId,
   640	                   left->identity.recommendationId,
   641	                   left->identity.proposalId) <
   642	            std::tie(right->identity.memberOrdinal,
   643	                   right->identity.materializationMemberId,
   644	                   right->identity.rankingMemberId,
   645	                   right->identity.recommendationId,
   646	                   right->identity.proposalId);
   647	    });
   648	
   649	    std::set<long long> materializationMemberIds;
   650	    std::set<long long> rankingMemberIds;
   651	    std::set<long long> recommendationIds;
   652	    std::set<long long> proposalIds;
   653	    MutableSummary counts;
   654	    std::vector<RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentMember> assessments;
   655	    assessments.reserve(ordered.size());
   656	    for (std::size_t index = 0; index < ordered.size(); ++index)
   657	    {
   658	        const auto& member = *ordered[index];
   659	        if (member.identity.memberOrdinal != static_cast<int>(index) + 1 ||
   660	            !materializationMemberIds
   661	                 .insert(member.identity.materializationMemberId)
   662	                 .second ||
   663	            !rankingMemberIds.insert(member.identity.rankingMemberId).second ||
   664	            !recommendationIds.insert(member.identity.recommendationId).second ||
   665	            !proposalIds.insert(member.identity.proposalId).second)
   666	            throw std::invalid_argument(
   667	                "recommendation_campaign_outcome_assessment_member_identity_set_invalid");
   668	        if (member.sourceEvidence &&
   669	            member.sourceEvidence->sourceExperimentId !=
   670	                member.identity.sourceExperimentId)
   671	            throw std::invalid_argument(
   672	                "recommendation_campaign_outcome_assessment_source_provenance_mismatch");
   673	        auto comparison = CompareRecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentEvidence(
   674	            member.sourceEvidence, member.resultEvidence);
   675	        CountComparison(counts, comparison);
   676	        assessments.emplace_back(member.identity, member.sourceEvidence,
   677	            member.resultEvidence, std::move(comparison));
   678	    }
   679	
   680	    RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentSummary summary(
   681	        static_cast<int>(assessments.size()), counts.comparableCount,
   682	        counts.contextChangedCount, counts.missingSourceEvidenceCount,
   683	        counts.missingResultEvidenceCount, counts.resultNotTerminalCount,
   684	        counts.metricUnavailableCount, counts.unsupportedComparisonCount,
   685	        counts.positiveDeltaCount, counts.zeroDeltaCount,
   686	        counts.negativeDeltaCount);
   687	    const std::string canonical = AssessmentCanonicalText(campaignIdentity,
   688	        materializationIdentity, summary, assessments);
   689	    RecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentIdentity assessmentIdentity(
   690	        kRecommendationCampaignOutcomeAssessmentContractVersion, canonical,
   691	        RecommendationCanonicalHash(canonical));
   692	    return {std::move(assessmentIdentity), campaignIdentity,
   693	        materializationIdentity, observedAt, std::move(summary),
   694	        std::move(assessments)};
   695	}
   696	
   697	#define EA_RECOMMENDATION_CAMPAIGN_OUTCOME_ASSESSMENT_TEXT_FUNCTION(          \
   698	    name, type, cases)                                                        \
   699	    std::string name(type value)                                              \
   700	    {                                                                         \
   701	        switch (value)                                                        \
   702	        {                                                                     \
   703	            cases                                                             \
   704	        }                                                                     \
   705	        throw std::invalid_argument(#name "_invalid");                       \
